<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Taking Cardy's crossing formula for critical Poisson–Voronoi percolation as an input, we prove a universal joint limit for the near-critical crossing thresholds of rational polygonal quadrilaterals under the monotone coupling. Conditional on the Poisson tessellation, the threshold law converges in probability over tessellations to the same law as on the triangular lattice. Each model is normalized by its own expected number of color-pivotal sites for a unit-square crossing.

- **claim-hyp-cardy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-hyp-cardy)): For every fixed bounded Jordan quad $Q=(D;a,b,c,d)$, the annealed probability of $C_Q$ in fair-colored full-plane Poisson–Voronoi percolation converges, as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$, to $$F(x)=\frac{\displaystyle\int_0^x [u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,du} {\displaystyle\int_0^1 [u(1-u)]^{-2/3}\,du}, \label{eq:cardy}$$ where a conformal map from $D$ to the upper half-plane sends $(a,b,c,d)$ to $(0,x,1,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: Input hypothesis: annealed crossing probabilities converge to Cardy's formula for every bounded Jordan quad.
- **claim-main-quenched-universality** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Assume Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:cardy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:cardy">1</a>. The expectations in <a href="#eq:requested-scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:requested-scales">[eq:requested-scales]</a> are finite and strictly positive for every mesh, and both scales tend to zero as their mesh tends to zero. There is a probability measure $\nu_\triangle$ on $\mathcal K$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(\tau_\delta^\triangle)\Longrightarrow\nu_\triangle \qquad(\delta\downarrow0). \label{eq:triangular-reference}$$ For the independently normalized Voronoi model, $$d_{\mathrm{BL}}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(\tau_\varepsilon^V\mid\eta_\varepsilon), \nu_\triangle\bigr) \xrightarrow[\varepsilon\downarrow0]{\mathbb P}0. \label{eq:main-quenched}$$ The conditional law averages all color marks and no geometry.
  - Explanation: Scales are finite, positive and vanishing; triangular threshold laws converge; the Voronoi conditional threshold law converges in probability to the same limit.
- **claim-uniform-arm-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arm-inputs)): For each model there are constants $C,c,\alpha,\beta>0$ and $g\in(1,2)$ such that, for $b_e\le r\le s\le R$, $$\begin{aligned} c(r/R)^g&\le A_e(r,R)\le C(r/R)^{1+\alpha},\label{eq:four-powers}\\ C^{-1}A_e(r,s)A_e(s,R)&\le A_e(r,R) \le C A_e(r,s)A_e(s,R).\label{eq:qm} \end{aligned}$$ The one-arm probability is at most $C(r/R)^\beta$, and the polychromatic five-arm probability is at most $C(r/R)^2$. The alternating three-arm probability in a half-plane in a standard orientation is at most $C(r/R)^2$.* *In the Poisson model, if $X_e(\eta;r,R)$ is the conditional probability of any of these specified arm events, then $$\label{eq:arm-second-moment} \bigl(\mathbb EX_e(\eta;r,R)^2\bigr)^{1/2} \le C\,\mathbb P(\text{the corresponding arm event}).$$ At fixed positive radii with $r\le R/2$, the conditional arm probabilities minus their annealed probabilities converge to zero in probability over the geometry. Both assertions apply to the half-plane events just stated. For triangular four arms one may choose $g\in(5/4,4/3)$.
  - Explanation: Uniform power-law bounds and quasi-multiplicativity for four-arm events down to the mesh, plus one-, five- and half-plane arm bounds.
- **claim-necessary-arms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pivotal-arms)): Compare two colored tilings which agree outside a ball and throughout an intervening annulus. If their crossing indicators for a polygon quad differ, an annulus contained in the quad and surrounding the change has four alternating arms. Near one straight side, at scales larger than the distance to that side and smaller than the distance to all vertices and other sides, the corresponding half-annulus has three alternating arms. At a vertex or a marked point, a one-arm test from a neighborhood of that point to a fixed larger distance is always necessary. Each assertion allows fixed-factor margins in its inner and outer radii and uses only colors in the agreement region. Other prescribed local changes may be present, provided that they are held fixed in the comparison.
  - Explanation: A differing crossing indicator under a local change forces alternating arms in the agreement annulus, with half-plane and corner versions.
- **claim-buffered-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-localization)): In a shell of radius $t\ge b_e$, one can choose a geometry event $\mathcal L_e(t)$, determined by the nuclei in a fixed enlargement of that shell, with $$\label{eq:screen-failure} \mathbb P(\mathcal L_e(t)^c)\le C\exp[-c(t/e)^2].$$ On this event the tiling and the vertex stars in a smaller shell are determined there, and every cell meeting that smaller shell has diameter and distance to its nucleus smaller than any prescribed fixed fraction of $t$. Fixed nuclei outside the enlarged shell do not affect this assertion. Screens with disjoint enlargements are independent; arm tests restricted by these screens and their color bits can likewise be used independently.* *Suppose a comparison requires four arms wherever the screens pass between radii $r,R$. Its necessary probe, allowing screen failures, has probability at most $C A_e(r,R)$. The part in which a screen fails at a scale $t\ge Mb_e$ is at most $$\label{eq:screen-relative-tail} \delta(M)A_e(r,R),\qquad \delta(M)\longrightarrow0,$$ when the smaller starting scale is $r\asymp b_e$; the constants may depend on the bounded factors implicit here. For a fixed positive starting radius $r$, the relative failure contribution tends to zero as $e\to0$. These conclusions also hold for probes consisting of four arms up to the distance from a straight side and three half-plane arms thereafter, once the half-plane estimate is bounded by $C A_e$.
  - Explanation: Screening events localize cells with super-exponentially small failure probability, and failed screens cost a vanishing relative share of an arm probe.
- **claim-quenched-cardy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-quenched-cardy)): For every fixed polygon quad $Q$, $$\mathbb P^{\eta_e}(C_Q)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Cardy}(Q) \quad\text{in probability over the Poisson geometry}.$$ The assertion holds simultaneously for every member of any fixed finite collection of polygon quads.
  - Explanation: Conditional crossing probabilities converge in probability over the geometry to the Cardy value, via Efron–Stein and arm bounds.
- **claim-good-sequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-sequences)): From every sequence $e\downarrow0$ in the Poisson model one can extract a subsequence and realize its geometries on a common probability space such that, almost surely, the following hold. The mesh tends to zero on every compact set; crossing probabilities of all rational polygon quads tend to their Cardy values; and the fixed-positive-scale arm estimates in Lemma <a href="#lem:arm-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:arm-inputs">3</a> hold for the conditional color laws, with a fixed constant slack, on any specified countable family of centers and radii. Conditional arm probabilities can also be compared to their mesh-dependent annealed values at these fixed scales.* *Any deterministic sequence satisfying these properties has the Cardy limit for Jordan quad approximations whose boundaries and marks converge in curve order to a Jordan quad. This includes graph-cycle approximations with fair interior vertices and terminal sides, and the literal closed-cell crossing convention. It also has uniformly positive crossing probabilities along any fixed finite collection of open tracks with compatible colors. The triangular sequence has all these properties deterministically.
  - Explanation: Subsequences exist along which meshes vanish, quad crossings converge to Cardy values and fixed-scale arm bounds hold.
- **claim-rectangle-color-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangle-colors)): Let a finite plane graph be drawn in a topological rectangle, with independent fair colors on its vertices. The probability of $k$ vertex-disjoint crossings between two opposite sides, with a specified ordered list of colors, does not depend on that list. The assertion remains valid after arbitrary vertex deletions. Uncolored fan terminals on the two target sides may be shared; disjointness and order are then imposed on the paths and their incident edges off the fan terminals.
  - Explanation: Probability of disjoint rectangle crossings does not depend on the prescribed color list.
- **claim-bulk-arms-any-color-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-color-arms)): There are $c_0,C>0$ such that, on a deterministic sequence and at fixed positive radii $r<R$, the probability of four disjoint bulk arms of any specified color order has limit superior at most $C(r/R)^{1+c_0}$. For six disjoint arms the corresponding bound is $C(r/R)^{2+c_0}$. The events may be taken between graph cycles in buffered circular or square annuli.
  - Explanation: Four and six disjoint bulk arms of any color order obey power bounds with exponents above one and two.
- **claim-chart-cardy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chart-cardy)): In the preceding setting the pulled-back triangulations have vanishing mesh. In a coordinate neighborhood of a compact open boundary interval on which $f$ is one-to-one, Jordan tests with fair interior paths have their ordinary Cardy limits. Their paths may terminate at neighbors of boundary vertices, but no prescribed boundary colors are counted as parts of those paths.* *More generally, a test whose full boundary has a nonsimple physical projection has the same conclusion whenever each of its two complementary crossings admits cropped target arcs and a connecting subdisk with Jordan image, with the cropped Cardy values approaching the two complementary values of the original test. The cropping is performed before target arcs are extended for a comparison.
  - Explanation: In conformal boundary charts the mesh vanishes and Jordan tests with fair interior paths keep their Cardy limits.
- **claim-separated-trims** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trim-arms)): For every $z>0$, one can choose a finite family of configurations of boxes on a trim curve, with fixed positive relative sizes and separations within each configuration, so that the following exception has limit superior probability at most $z$. The exception is that two distinct simple arms traversing a wider collar approach each other near the trim curve too closely for each retained endpoint to have its own box and a tenfold enlargement avoided by all the other arms. In a half-annulus also exclude visits too close to the two endpoints of the semicircle on the base. The conclusion applies to two arms in a half-annulus and to four arms, with fixed cyclic order, in a full annulus. It controls extra approaches of the arms to the endpoint boxes, as well as their first trim locations. The exception may be chosen as a union of local one-arm and four-arm tests in a fixed wider collar, whose width does not depend on $z$.
  - Explanation: A finite family of box configurations on a trim curve makes the close-approach exception small.
- **claim-local-guarded-extension** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-guarded-extension)): Fix the endpoint boxes and separations of Lemma <a href="#lem:trim-arms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trim-arms">11</a>, and prescribe separated destination intervals or patches on the other side of fixed-ratio extension regions, in the same order. In a full annulus a specified cyclic offset is allowed. Let $H$ be the event that proper retained arms with specified colors exist between the trim curves, with endpoints in these boxes, and with every other arm avoiding each box’s reserved enlargement. Require that each extension region overlap the retained region only in the corresponding reserved enlargement, where only the arm of its prescribed color is allowed. The arms can be extended to the destinations, with circuits or transverse crossings at their ends available for further connections, at a probability cost bounded below by a positive constant: $$\label{eq:local-extension} \mathbb P(H\text{ and the prescribed extensions})\ge c\,\mathbb P(H).$$ The constant depends only on the normalized box and track layouts: it is uniform for similarity copies of a fixed finite family of layouts, including layouts in admissible coordinate charts. The bound holds for all sufficiently fine meshes of a deterministic sequence, with the mesh threshold allowed to depend on the fixed scales and chart. It remains valid with additional restrictions on bits outside the extension regions.
  - Explanation: Retained arms extend to prescribed destinations at probability cost bounded below by a positive constant.
- **claim-free-boundary-arms** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-arms)): In the coordinate patches of Lemma <a href="#lem:chart-cardy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:chart-cardy">10</a>, let $A_{j,n}^+(r,R)$ denote the probability of $j$ disjoint arms across a half-annulus, of any specified color order, using only fair interior vertices. With fixed positive radii, constant-factor margins, and room within the patch, $$\label{eq:boundary-arms} \begin{aligned} \limsup_n A_{1,n}^+(r,R)&\le C(r/R)^{1/3},& \limsup_n A_{2,n}^+(r,R)&\le C(r/R),\\ \limsup_n A_{3,n}^+(r,R)&\le C(r/R)^{4/3}.&& \end{aligned}$$ Base vertices of prescribed boundary color are excluded. The same convention applies to a crosscut at which an exploration is stopped before querying its vertices. In a full-plane tiling, arms allowed to approach a straight or smooth test boundary are covered by slightly extending the coordinate half-plane, with the resulting errors absorbed at the inner radius.
  - Explanation: Free one-, two- and three-arm half-annulus probabilities decay with exponents one third, one and four thirds.
- **claim-pivotal-scale-finite** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pivotal-scale)): For every mesh, the expected number of color-pivotal sites for $Q_*$ and for $$Q_0=[-1,1]\times[-1/2,1/2]$$ with left–right target sides is finite and strictly positive. At criticality, $$\label{eq:aux-pivotal-scale} \mathbb EN_e(Q_0)\asymp m_e A_e(b_e,1).$$ Consequently, with $$\label{eq:aux-scale-def} a_e=A_e(b_e,1),\qquad q_e=\frac1{\mathbb EN_e(Q_0)},$$ we have $q_e\asymp(m_ea_e)^{-1}$ and $q_e\to0$.
  - Explanation: Expected pivotal count is finite, positive and comparable to site density times the four-arm probability, so its reciprocal vanishes.
- **claim-chordal-exploration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-chordal)): Let graph disks on a deterministic sequence as above approximate a bounded Jordan domain $D$, with switches converging to distinct boundary points $a,b$. Give their interiors independent fair colors and their two boundary arcs the prescribed opposite colors. The exploration from $a$ to $b$ converges in law, in the curve metric, to chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_6$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$. This conclusion holds for every such converging sequence of disks and marks.
  - Explanation: Explorations on graph disks converge in law in the curve metric to chordal SLE_6.
- **claim-revealed-faces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-revealed-faces)): The revealed graph is two-connected. Each of its faces that contains unqueried sites has a simple, internally chord-free site cycle as boundary. For a nonempty explored band, such a cycle shares at most one interval with the original boundary cycle; the interval may be empty or a single vertex. After a completed exploration every such face has a monochromatic boundary. If an exploration is stopped with its outgoing edge facing the unqueried face containing the target switch, that face has exactly two switches, namely the target and the outgoing edge.
  - Explanation: The revealed graph is two-connected; unqueried faces have simple chord-free boundary cycles.
- **claim-exploration-tightness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exploration-tightness)): Exploration paths are tight in the curve metric. The same holds jointly for boundaries of faces produced by a completed exploration and for the two colored arcs of a stopped target face. At any fixed positive scale their numbers of annular traversals are tight. These statements hold in the disk coordinates just described and, by uniform continuity of the conformal maps, in physical coordinates.
  - Explanation: Exploration paths and face boundaries are tight in the curve metric.
- **claim-exploration-contacts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exploration-contacts)): In a subsequential curve limit, the exploration has no interior triple point, no double point on the original boundary, and no second visit to either switch. Its interior double points avoid every fixed line segment. It has no nonconstant interval lying on the boundary. Moreover a discrete excursion whose bank shadows avoid a specified boundary bank cannot acquire an intermediate contact with that bank in the limit, provided the positions immediately before and after that contact make positive excursions from it.* *For a random monochromatic arc of a face boundary, distinct interior visits to the same point are excluded. On an original boundary interval of its own color it cannot have an intermediate contact between positive excursions. A face of positive limiting diameter cannot attach to a boundary interval of its own color through an interval collapsing to a point, including a single-vertex attachment. These statements also hold with the colors reversed.
  - Explanation: Limits have no interior triple points, boundary double points or second switch visits.
- **claim-face-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-face-convergence)): After a completed exploration, every nonconstant subsequential limit of a remaining face boundary is a Jordan curve. Its interior is a component of the complement of the limiting exploration in the initial domain. Faces of diameter at least any fixed $r>0$ have tight numbers, converge without surplus faces, and retain their colors and their intervals of attachment to the original boundary. Thresholds on diameters may be chosen outside the atoms of the limiting sizes.
  - Explanation: Limits of remaining face boundaries are Jordan curves bounding complement components of the limiting exploration.
- **claim-stopped-target-domain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stopped-domain)): At a stop separated from the two endpoints of $L$, the outgoing edge faces the unqueried component containing the target. In a coupled subsequential limit its boundary consists of two individually simple colored banks, joining the new tip to the target, in prime-end order. In the current coordinates the target domain is the union of the back, two Jordan side domains in the front, and their two open attaching intervals on $L$. The two colored side arcs meet $L$ only at their prescribed endpoints, one of which is the hit point. Normalized conformal maps onto the new target domains converge uniformly on their closed parameter disks. The same is true of the composed maps into the original physical domain.
  - Explanation: The stopped target face decomposes into back and two side domains, with uniformly convergent conformal maps.
- **claim-stopped-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stopped-kernel)): The joint limiting law of the hit on $L$ and the two side domains of Lemma <a href="#lem:stopped-domain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stopped-domain">20</a> is uniquely determined by the scalar Cardy limits. For a semicircle cut in coordinates with start $0$ and target $\infty$, it is the law of chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_6$ stopped when it first reaches that semicircle.
  - Explanation: Scalar Cardy limits determine the hit and side-domain law, equal to stopped chordal SLE_6.
- **claim-new-tip-no-disconnection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-new-tip-disconnection)): Let a continuous chordal curve in a Jordan disk generate its filled target hulls. At a finite time before its first visit to the target, at which its tip is interior and has not been visited previously, no point at positive distance from the trace up to that time and from the initial boundary can become disconnected from the target for the first time.
  - Explanation: A freshly visited interior tip cannot cause a first disconnection of a point from the target.
- **claim-no-hidden-excursions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-hidden-excursions)): Every subsequential limit of the exploration in Proposition <a href="#prop:chordal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:chordal">15</a> has the law of chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_6$ as an ordered curve.
  - Explanation: Sampled target domains pin down the curve: subsequential limits are chordal SLE_6 as ordered curves.
- **claim-virtual-restoration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-virtual-restoration)): Restoring a virtual boundary bank pairs its excursions with explorations of the incident unknown faces according to their order along the bank. For every fixed positive diameter cutoff, the excursions, the incident faces used to complete them, and their completed oriented loops converge jointly. No macroscopic loop is produced in the limit solely by excursions or incident faces of vanishing size at this step. Conditional on the exposed boundaries, all still unknown interior colors remain independent and fair.
  - Explanation: Restoring a virtual bank pairs excursions with face explorations in bank order, converging jointly without surplus loops.
- **claim-oriented-loops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-loops)): In a Jordan disk with monochromatic boundary, along every deterministic sequence of tilings under consideration, the critical interface loops converge to the Camia–Newman $\mathrm{SLE}_6$ loop construction. For each positive cutoff chosen outside the atoms of limiting loop diameters, the loops above that cutoff can be matched in the curve metric, including their orientation, multiplicity, and order of excursions. There are no surplus macroscopic loops. The convergence holds jointly for any finite number of independent color replicas on the same fixed geometry.
  - Explanation: Critical interface loops converge to the Camia–Newman SLE_6 loop construction, jointly for replicas.
- **claim-boundary-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-pairing)): Suppose finitely many disjoint interfaces, with their two bank colors, cut a Jordan quad, avoid its marks, and cross its boundary whenever they meet it. If an interface crosscut joins $S_1$ to $S_3$, the black bank supplies a black crossing. If one joins $S_2$ to $S_4$, the white bank supplies a complementary white crossing. If neither type occurs, there is a complementary region incident to all four sides; its color determines the crossing. Whenever a crosscut joins the two sides incident to a mark, the orientation of the outermost such corner cut determines this central color.
  - Explanation: A quad crossing is read from interface crosscut pairings and the outermost corner cut orientation.
- **claim-forced-continuity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forced-continuity)): For any fixed finite family of polygon crossing tests with the square prescriptions above, the probability that a test changes under a sufficiently small polygonal displacement of its sides, marks, or prescribed squares tends to zero, uniformly in the mesh-first limit. The displacements may change the centers and positive side lengths of the squares, provided the stated separations persist. The same conclusion holds simultaneously for any fixed finite collection of prescription choices on the same colors.
  - Explanation: Prescribed-square tests are continuous under small polygonal displacements, uniformly in the mesh-first limit.
- **claim-free-segments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-segments)): Discard squares left unchanged in the test and squares outside its quad. Construct a graph approximation to the middle quad with the remaining middle squares removed. Give its vertices the original fair colors. Fill each square hole by a fan from one new vertex having that square’s prescribed color, and attach uncolored terminals to the two target arcs. On the event that the original modified configuration crosses, this graph has a black terminal path for all sufficiently small meshes. It has such a path whose portions between consecutive terminals or fan vertices form at most $J+1$ free paths. Their joint laws, restricted to the crossing event, are tight in the curve metric. Every subsequential limiting free path is simple after constant parameter intervals are removed.
  - Explanation: A terminal black path splits into boundedly many tight free segments with simple limits.
- **claim-forced-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forced-transfer)): Let two deterministic good sequences be coupled so that their ordinary critical polygon crossing indicators converge jointly to the same indicators for a countable family containing the rational polygons. For any fixed prescribed-square test, the probability of a crossing in the first sequence and no crossing in the final favorable perturbation of the second tends to zero. The conclusion holds for finitely many tests and for independent color replicas.
  - Explanation: A prescribed-square crossing in one sequence forces a favorable-perturbation crossing in the other asymptotically.
- **claim-joint-critical-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-critical-tests)): Fix finitely many polygon quads and finitely many closed squares of positive side length, with the squares mutually separated and separated from every quad boundary. For any finite collection of test specifications, the joint law of their exact closed-cell crossing indicators has a common limit along every deterministic good sequence of either model. The limit is continuous in the centers and positive side lengths of the squares throughout the open range where the stated separations hold, and under sufficiently small marked polygonal deformations of the quads. The conclusion remains valid when the centers and side lengths converge to such an admissible fixed configuration as the mesh tends to zero.* *For Poisson–Voronoi percolation the conditional law given $\eta_e$ converges in probability, in total variation on this finite Boolean space, to the same deterministic limiting law. With any fixed number of conditionally independent fair-color replicas on one Poisson geometry, the limiting law is that of independent copies of the same critical configuration, with all the prescribed tests performed in their specified replicas. In particular, two replicas sharing their geometry have the same joint critical limit as two independent triangular replicas.
  - Explanation: Joint prescribed-square crossing indicators have a common continuous limit in both models, with independent replicas.
- **claim-expansion-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-expansion-bound)): Fix a box $B$, finitely many polygonal crossing tests, and $R\leq2$ replica labels. Let $g\in(1,2)$ be an exponent in the lower four-arm bound of Lemma <a href="#lem:arm-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:arm-inputs">3</a>. There is a constant $C$, depending only on this data, such that, for every $k\geq1$, every choice of nonempty $I_i$, and centered weights $w_{i,r}$, $$\frac{(m_eq_e)^k}{k!} \int_{B^k_{\ne}} \|K_e(F;\mathbf x,\mathbf I,\mathbf w)\| \,d\sigma_e^{\otimes k}(\mathbf x) \leq C^k k^{-(1-g/2)k} \prod_{i,r\in I_i}\\|w_{i,r}\\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}}, \label{eq:coefficient-bound}$$ uniformly for all sufficiently small meshes and all $\|F\|\leq1$ of the specified crossing indicators. Consequently the absolute sum in <a href="#eq:site-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:site-expansion">[eq:site-expansion]</a> is bounded by $\sum_{k\geq1}C_T^k k^{-(1-g/2)k}<\infty$ when all times have absolute value at most $T$. For two replicas, the total absolute contribution of terms with $\|I_i\|=2$ for at least one $i$ is $O_T(q_e)$.
  - Explanation: Each order of the site expansion is bounded by a factorially decaying majorant, giving absolute summability.
- **claim-location-uniform-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-location-ui)): Fix $k$, the polygonal test family, $R\leq2$, and an upper bound on all the variation norms in <a href="#eq:site-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:site-kernel">[eq:site-kernel]</a>. There are $p>1$ and $C_k<\infty$ such that, after extending the kernel by zero to coincident tuples, $$\int_{B^k} \left\|(m_eq_e)^kK_e(F;\mathbf x,\mathbf I,\mathbf w)\right\|^p \,d\sigma_e^{\otimes k}(\mathbf x) \leq C_k. \label{eq:kernel-lp}$$ Let $Z$ be the union of all tested polygon boundaries and $\partial B$. For $\rho>0$ let $\mathcal B_\rho\subset B^k$ consist of tuples for which $\|x_i-x_j\|<\rho$ for some $i\ne j$ or $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x_i,Z)<\rho$ for some $i$. Then $$\lim_{\rho\downarrow0}\limsup_{e\downarrow0} (m_eq_e)^k\int_{\mathcal B_\rho} \|K_e(F;\mathbf x,\mathbf I,\mathbf w)\| \,d\sigma_e^{\otimes k}(\mathbf x)=0. \label{eq:location-cutoff}$$ The conclusions are uniform in the indicated tests and weights. They remain true after omitting the geometry cutoff $\Gamma_e$ from the definition of the kernel.
  - Explanation: Normalized kernels are bounded in L^p for some p above one; clusters and near-boundary positions carry vanishing mass.
- **claim-square-transmits-pivotal-change** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-disk-lower)): There is $c>0$ such that, for every sufficiently small fixed $h>0$ and all sufficiently small $e$ depending on $h$, $$D_e(h)\geq c a_{e,h}. \label{eq:disk-lower}$$ The same assertion holds for the one-time square-insertion kernel of a fixed square or rectangle crossing when its center varies in a sufficiently small open neighborhood of the center of that test. The constant is uniform over that neighborhood.
  - Explanation: A forced small square transmits a pivotal change with probability at least a constant times the four-arm cost.
- **claim-signed-relative-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-replacement)): Under the preceding assumptions, fix the types $t_2,\ldots,t_k$ and put $$K_s=K_e^{(s,t_2,\ldots,t_k)}(F;\boldsymbol x,\boldsymbol w),\qquad K_d=K_e^{(d,t_2,\ldots,t_k)}(F;\boldsymbol x,\boldsymbol w),\qquad P_e=\prod_{i=2}^k \gamma_e(t_i).$$ The empty product is $1$. In either model, $$\lim_{h\downarrow0}\limsup_{e\downarrow0} \sup_{\boldsymbol x} \frac{\|K_sD_e(h)-K_dS_e\|}{P_e a_e a_{e,h}}=0. \label{eq:signed-relative-replacement}$$ The supremum is over the fixed compact separated range, restricted to lattice sites in the triangular model; in Poisson insertion integrals, null general-position exceptions are omitted. The assertion is uniform for bounded total variations of the centered weights.
  - Explanation: Replacing a site insertion by a square insertion in a signed kernel product has vanishing relative error.
- **claim-guarded-arms-lower** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-guarded-extension)): Fix sufficiently large end margins and four separated target patches at each end of an annulus of scales $l<L$, in matching alternating cyclic order. Assume $L/l$ exceeds a constant determined by these end layouts and margins. The ratio may be arbitrarily large but is fixed before the mesh limit. On typical local geometries, the conditional probability of four alternating arms with guarded endpoints in those patches is at least $$c\,A_e(l,L). \label{eq:guarded-arms-lower}$$ The constant depends on the prescribed end layouts and margins, but is independent of the intermediate ratio. In the endpoint overlap regions, requirements use each color coordinate monotonically in its prescribed color order, with no opposite-color requirement on the same coordinate. The entire event is measurable in the annulus with its end margins.
  - Explanation: Four alternating arms with guarded landings cost at least a constant times the four-arm probability.
- **claim-necklace-sleeve** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-necklace-sleeve)): There is a fixed sufficiently large annulus ratio for which the following event has conditional probability at least $c_0>0$ on typical local geometry: four alternating arms cross the annulus, have guarded endpoints in prescribed patches at both ends, and meet a necklace in its middle, one arm through each corresponding run. All additional requirements beyond the guarded arm event are supported away from the endpoint overlap regions.
  - Explanation: Guarded alternating arms meeting a four-run necklace occur with positive conditional probability.
- **claim-necklace-bit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-necklace-bit)): On a successful seam event, let $b\in\{0,1\}$ record whether the two black runs of its inner necklace are connected inside that necklace. The same connection test at its outer necklace equals $b$. For every active-label crossing whose quad contains the entire closed trial disk, the dependence on the interior is only through $b$. A quad disjoint from the trial disk is unaffected. These are the only two cases for the fixed tests in the construction.
  - Explanation: On a successful seam the interior influences exterior crossings only through one connection bit.
- **claim-one-trial-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-trial)): For the preceding fixed layouts and tolerances, there is $C_*<\infty$, independent of the number of trials, their scales, and sufficiently small mesh, such that $$\mathbb P(E\cap N\cap B_J^c) \leq C_*\, \mathbb P\left(E\cap U_J\cap\bigcap_{I\ne J}U_I^c\right) \label{eq:one-trial-comparison}$$ for every trial $J$.
  - Explanation: Failure of all trials is bounded by a constant times success of exactly one specified trial.
- **claim-common-calibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-common-calibration)): There is a constant $c\in(0,\infty)$ such that $c_e\to c$ in both models. Moreover, for each fixed degree, assignment of one replica label to each insertion, and finite signed crossing test, its normalized all-site kernel integral has a limit common to the two models.
  - Explanation: The site-to-square ratio converges to one positive constant common to both models.
- **claim-square-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-square-normalization)): There is a common constant $B\in(0,\infty)$ such that, in each model, $$q_e\mathbb EN_e(Q_*)\longrightarrow B, \qquad r_e=\frac{q_e}{B+o(1)}. \label{eq:square-normalization}$$ In particular, both prescribed scales tend to zero.
  - Explanation: The normalized unit-square pivotal count converges to a common positive constant.
- **claim-nearcritical-finite-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nearcritical-tests)): Choose finitely many quads in $\mathcal Q_{\mathrm{rat}}$ and finitely many real parameters. Their jointly coupled crossing indicators, with the prescribed scales $r_e$, have a limiting law common to the two models. The assertion holds for one color replica and for two independent color replicas conditional on the same geometry. Every such limiting probability is continuous in the real parameter vector, and the convergence remains valid for convergent parameter vectors.
  - Explanation: Finitely many quad crossings at finitely many parameters have a common limit law in both models.

There is a probability measure $\\nu_\\triangle$ on $\\mathcal K$ such that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(\\tau_\\delta^\\triangle)\\Longrightarrow\\nu_\\triangle\n \\qquad(\\delta\\downarrow0).\n \\label{eq:triangular-reference}$$ For the independently normalized Voronoi model, $$d_{\\mathrm{BL}}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(\\tau_\\varepsilon^V\\mid\\eta_\\varepsilon),\n                 \\nu_\\triangle\\bigr)\n \\xrightarrow[\\varepsilon\\downarrow0]{\\mathbb P}0.\n \\label{eq:main-quenched}$$ The conditional law averages all color marks and no geometry.",
      "explanation": "Scales are finite, positive and vanishing; triangular threshold laws converge; the Voronoi conditional threshold law converges in probability to the same limit.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arm-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "For each model there are constants $C,c,\\alpha,\\beta>0$ and $g\\in(1,2)$ such that, for $b_e\\le r\\le s\\le R$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n c(r/R)^g&\\le A_e(r,R)\\le C(r/R)^{1+\\alpha},\\label{eq:four-powers}\\\\\n C^{-1}A_e(r,s)A_e(s,R)&\\le A_e(r,R)\n       \\le C A_e(r,s)A_e(s,R).\\label{eq:qm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The one-arm probability is at most $C(r/R)^\\beta$, and the polychromatic five-arm probability is at most $C(r/R)^2$. The alternating three-arm probability in a half-plane in a standard orientation is at most $C(r/R)^2$.*\n\n\n*In the Poisson model, if $X_e(\\eta;r,R)$ is the conditional probability of any of these specified arm events, then $$\\label{eq:arm-second-moment}\n \\bigl(\\mathbb EX_e(\\eta;r,R)^2\\bigr)^{1/2}\n       \\le C\\,\\mathbb P(\\text{the corresponding arm event}).$$ At fixed positive radii with $r\\le R/2$, the conditional arm probabilities minus their annealed probabilities converge to zero in probability over the geometry. Both assertions apply to the half-plane events just stated. For triangular four arms one may choose $g\\in(5/4,4/3)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pivotal-arms",
        "exact_quote": "Compare two colored tilings which agree outside a ball and throughout an intervening annulus. If their crossing indicators for a polygon quad differ, an annulus contained in the quad and surrounding the change has four alternating arms. Near one straight side, at scales larger than the distance to that side and smaller than the distance to all vertices and other sides, the corresponding half-annulus has three alternating arms. At a vertex or a marked point, a one-arm test from a neighborhood of that point to a fixed larger distance is always necessary. Each assertion allows fixed-factor margins in its inner and outer radii and uses only colors in the agreement region. Other prescribed local changes may be present, provided that they are held fixed in the comparison.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pivotal-arms",
          "exact_quote": "Compare two colored tilings which agree outside a ball and throughout an intervening annulus. At a vertex or a marked point, a one-arm test from a neighborhood of that point to a fixed larger distance is always necessary. Each assertion allows fixed-factor margins in its inner and outer radii and uses only colors in the agreement region.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
        "exact_quote": "In a shell of radius $t\\ge b_e$, one can choose a geometry event $\\mathcal L_e(t)$, determined by the nuclei in a fixed enlargement of that shell, with $$\\label{eq:screen-failure}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal L_e(t)^c)\\le C\\exp[-c(t/e)^2].$$ On this event the tiling and the vertex stars in a smaller shell are determined there, and every cell meeting that smaller shell has diameter and distance to its nucleus smaller than any prescribed fixed fraction of $t$. Fixed nuclei outside the enlarged shell do not affect this assertion. Screens with disjoint enlargements are independent; arm tests restricted by these screens and their color bits can likewise be used independently.*\n\n*Suppose a comparison requires four arms wherever the screens pass between radii $r,R$. Its necessary probe, allowing screen failures, has probability at most $C A_e(r,R)$. The part in which a screen fails at a scale $t\\ge Mb_e$ is at most $$\\label{eq:screen-relative-tail}\n \\delta(M)A_e(r,R),\\qquad \\delta(M)\\longrightarrow0,$$ when the smaller starting scale is $r\\asymp b_e$; the constants may depend on the bounded factors implicit here. For a fixed positive starting radius $r$, the relative failure contribution tends to zero as $e\\to0$. These conclusions also hold for probes consisting of four arms up to the distance from a straight side and three half-plane arms thereafter, once the half-plane estimate is bounded by $C A_e$.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
          "exact_quote": "In a shell of radius $t\\ge b_e$, one can choose a geometry event $\\mathcal L_e(t)$, determined by the nuclei in a fixed enlargement of that shell, with $$\\label{eq:screen-failure}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal L_e(t)^c)\\le C\\exp[-c(t/e)^2].$$ On this event the tiling and the vertex stars in a smaller shell are determined there, and every cell meeting that smaller shell has diameter and distance to its nucleus smaller than any prescribed fixed fraction of $t$. Fixed nuclei outside the enlarged shell do not affect this assertion. Screens with disjoint enlargements are independent; arm tests restricted by these screens and their color bits can likewise be used independently.*\n\n*Suppose a comparison requires four arms wherever the screens pass between radii $r,R$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-sequences",
        "exact_quote": "From every sequence $e\\downarrow0$ in the Poisson model one can extract a subsequence and realize its geometries on a common probability space such that, almost surely, the following hold. The mesh tends to zero on every compact set; crossing probabilities of all rational polygon quads tend to their Cardy values; and the fixed-positive-scale arm estimates in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:arm-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:arm-inputs\">3</a> hold for the conditional color laws, with a fixed constant slack, on any specified countable family of centers and radii. Conditional arm probabilities can also be compared to their mesh-dependent annealed values at these fixed scales.*\n\n*Any deterministic sequence satisfying these properties has the Cardy limit for Jordan quad approximations whose boundaries and marks converge in curve order to a Jordan quad. This includes graph-cycle approximations with fair interior vertices and terminal sides, and the literal closed-cell crossing convention. It also has uniformly positive crossing probabilities along any fixed finite collection of open tracks with compatible colors. The triangular sequence has all these properties deterministically.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-sequences",
          "exact_quote": "From every sequence $e\\downarrow0$ in the Poisson model one can extract a subsequence and realize its geometries on a common probability space such that, almost surely, the following hold. Conditional arm probabilities can also be compared to their mesh-dependent annealed values at these fixed scales.*\n\n*Any deterministic sequence satisfying these properties has the Cardy limit for Jordan quad approximations whose boundaries and marks converge in curve order to a Jordan quad. It also has uniformly positive crossing probabilities along any fixed finite collection of open tracks with compatible colors. The triangular sequence has all these properties deterministically.",
          "source_id": "PM-224.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rectangle-colors",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite plane graph be drawn in a topological rectangle, with independent fair colors on its vertices. The probability of $k$ vertex-disjoint crossings between two opposite sides, with a specified ordered list of colors, does not depend on that list.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-cardy",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding setting the pulled-back triangulations have vanishing mesh. In a coordinate neighborhood of a compact open boundary interval on which $f$ is one-to-one, Jordan tests with fair interior paths have their ordinary Cardy limits. Their paths may terminate at neighbors of boundary vertices, but no prescribed boundary colors are counted as parts of those paths.*\n\n*More generally, a test whose full boundary has a nonsimple physical projection has the same conclusion whenever each of its two complementary crossings admits cropped target arcs and a connecting subdisk with Jordan image, with the cropped Cardy values approaching the two complementary values of the original test. The cropping is performed before target arcs are extended for a comparison.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "In the preceding setting the pulled-back triangulations have vanishing mesh.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trim-arms",
        "exact_quote": "For every $z>0$, one can choose a finite family of configurations of boxes on a trim curve, with fixed positive relative sizes and separations within each configuration, so that the following exception has limit superior probability at most $z$. The exception is that two distinct simple arms traversing a wider collar approach each other near the trim curve too closely for each retained endpoint to have its own box and a tenfold enlargement avoided by all the other arms. In a half-annulus also exclude visits too close to the two endpoints of the semicircle on the base. The conclusion applies to two arms in a half-annulus and to four arms, with fixed cyclic order, in a full annulus. It controls extra approaches of the arms to the endpoint boxes, as well as their first trim locations. The exception may be chosen as a union of local one-arm and four-arm tests in a fixed wider collar, whose width does not depend on $z$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-guarded-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the endpoint boxes and separations of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:trim-arms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:trim-arms\">11</a>, and prescribe separated destination intervals or patches on the other side of fixed-ratio extension regions, in the same order. In a full annulus a specified cyclic offset is allowed. Let $H$ be the event that proper retained arms with specified colors exist between the trim curves, with endpoints in these boxes, and with every other arm avoiding each box’s reserved enlargement. Require that each extension region overlap the retained region only in the corresponding reserved enlargement, where only the arm of its prescribed color is allowed. The arms can be extended to the destinations, with circuits or transverse crossings at their ends available for further connections, at a probability cost bounded below by a positive constant: $$\\label{eq:local-extension}\n \\mathbb P(H\\text{ and the prescribed extensions})\\ge c\\,\\mathbb P(H).$$ The constant depends only on the normalized box and track layouts: it is uniform for similarity copies of a fixed finite family of layouts, including layouts in admissible coordinate charts. The bound holds for all sufficiently fine meshes of a deterministic sequence, with the mesh threshold allowed to depend on the fixed scales and chart. It remains valid with additional restrictions on bits outside the extension regions.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-arms",
        "exact_quote": "In the coordinate patches of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:chart-cardy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:chart-cardy\">10</a>, let $A_{j,n}^+(r,R)$ denote the probability of $j$ disjoint arms across a half-annulus, of any specified color order, using only fair interior vertices. With fixed positive radii, constant-factor margins, and room within the patch, $$\\label{eq:boundary-arms}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\limsup_n A_{1,n}^+(r,R)&\\le C(r/R)^{1/3},&\n \\limsup_n A_{2,n}^+(r,R)&\\le C(r/R),\\\\\n \\limsup_n A_{3,n}^+(r,R)&\\le C(r/R)^{4/3}.&&\n \\end{aligned}$$ Base vertices of prescribed boundary color are excluded. The same convention applies to a crosscut at which an exploration is stopped before querying its vertices. In a full-plane tiling, arms allowed to approach a straight or smooth test boundary are covered by slightly extending the coordinate half-plane, with the resulting errors absorbed at the inner radius.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-chordal",
        "exact_quote": "Let graph disks on a deterministic sequence as above approximate a bounded Jordan domain $D$, with switches converging to distinct boundary points $a,b$. Give their interiors independent fair colors and their two boundary arcs the prescribed opposite colors. The exploration from $a$ to $b$ converges in law, in the curve metric, to chordal $\\mathrm{SLE}_6$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$. This conclusion holds for every such converging sequence of disks and marks.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-revealed-faces",
        "exact_quote": "The revealed graph is two-connected. Each of its faces that contains unqueried sites has a simple, internally chord-free site cycle as boundary. For a nonempty explored band, such a cycle shares at most one interval with the original boundary cycle; the interval may be empty or a single vertex. After a completed exploration every such face has a monochromatic boundary. If an exploration is stopped with its outgoing edge facing the unqueried face containing the target switch, that face has exactly two switches, namely the target and the outgoing edge.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exploration-contacts",
        "exact_quote": "In a subsequential curve limit, the exploration has no interior triple point, no double point on the original boundary, and no second visit to either switch. Its interior double points avoid every fixed line segment. It has no nonconstant interval lying on the boundary. Moreover a discrete excursion whose bank shadows avoid a specified boundary bank cannot acquire an intermediate contact with that bank in the limit, provided the positions immediately before and after that contact make positive excursions from it.*\n\n*For a random monochromatic arc of a face boundary, distinct interior visits to the same point are excluded. On an original boundary interval of its own color it cannot have an intermediate contact between positive excursions. A face of positive limiting diameter cannot attach to a boundary interval of its own color through an interval collapsing to a point, including a single-vertex attachment. These statements also hold with the colors reversed.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stopped-domain",
        "exact_quote": "At a stop separated from the two endpoints of $L$, the outgoing edge faces the unqueried component containing the target. In a coupled subsequential limit its boundary consists of two individually simple colored banks, joining the new tip to the target, in prime-end order. In the current coordinates the target domain is the union of the back, two Jordan side domains in the front, and their two open attaching intervals on $L$. The two colored side arcs meet $L$ only at their prescribed endpoints, one of which is the hit point.         "exact_quote": "Restoring a virtual boundary bank pairs its excursions with explorations of the incident unknown faces according to their order along the bank.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-pairing",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose finitely many disjoint interfaces, with their two bank colors, cut a Jordan quad, avoid its marks, and cross its boundary whenever they meet it. If an interface crosscut joins $S_1$ to $S_3$, the black bank supplies a black crossing. If one joins $S_2$ to $S_4$, the white bank supplies a complementary white crossing. If neither type occurs, there is a complementary region incident to all four sides; its color determines the crossing. Whenever a crosscut joins the two sides incident to a mark, the orientation of the outermost such corner cut determines this central color.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-segments",
        "exact_quote": "Discard squares left unchanged in the test and squares outside its quad. Construct a graph approximation to the middle quad with the remaining middle squares removed. Give its vertices the original fair colors. Fill each square hole by a fan from one new vertex having that square’s prescribed color, and attach uncolored terminals to the two target arcs. On the event that the original modified configuration crosses, this graph has a black terminal path for all sufficiently small meshes. It has such a path whose portions between consecutive terminals or fan vertices form at most $J+1$ free paths. Their joint laws, restricted to the crossing event, are tight in the curve metric. Every subsequential limiting free path is simple after constant parameter intervals are removed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-critical-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many polygon quads and finitely many closed squares of positive side length, with the squares mutually separated and separated from every quad boundary. For any finite collection of test specifications, the joint law of their exact closed-cell crossing indicators has a common limit along every deterministic good sequence of either model. The limit is continuous in the centers and positive side lengths of the squares throughout the open range where the stated separations hold, and under sufficiently small marked polygonal deformations of the quads. The conclusion remains valid when the centers and side lengths converge to such an admissible fixed configuration as the mesh tends to zero.*\n\n*For Poisson–Voronoi percolation the conditional law given $\\eta_e$ converges in probability, in total variation on this finite Boolean space, to the same deterministic limiting law. With any fixed number of conditionally independent fair-color replicas on one Poisson geometry, the limiting law is that of independent copies of the same critical configuration, with all the prescribed tests performed in their specified replicas. In particular, two replicas sharing their geometry have the same joint critical limit as two independent triangular replicas.",
        "source_id": "PM-224.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-expansion-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a box $B$, finitely many polygonal crossing tests, and $R\\leq2$ replica labels. Let $g\\in(1,2)$ be an exponent in the lower four-arm bound of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:arm-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:arm-inputs\">3</a>. There is a constant $C$, depending only on this data, such that, for every $k\\geq1$, every choice of nonempty $I_i$, and centered weights $w_{i,r}$, $$\\frac{(m_eq_e)^k}{k!}\n   \\int_{B^k_{\\ne}}\n       |K_e(F;\\mathbf x,\\mathbf I,\\mathbf w)|\n                    \\,d\\sigma_e^{\\otimes k}(\\mathbf x)\n \\leq C^k k^{-(1-g/2)k}\n             \\prod_{i,r\\in I_i}\\|w_{i,r}\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}},\n \\label{eq:coefficient-bound}$$ uniformly for all sufficiently small meshes and all $|F|\\leq1$ of the specified crossing indicators. Consequently the absolute sum in <a href=\"#eq:site-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:site-expansion\">[eq:site-expansion]</a> is bounded by $\\sum_{k\\geq1}C_T^k k^{-(1-g/2)k}<\\infty$ when all times have absolute value at most $T$. For two replicas, the total absolute contribution of terms with $|I_i|=2$ for at least one $i$ is $O_T(q_e)$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-guarded-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Fix sufficiently large end margins and four separated target patches at each end of an annulus of scales $l<L$, in matching alternating cyclic order. Assume $L/l$ exceeds a constant determined by these end layouts and margins. The ratio may be arbitrarily large but is fixed before the mesh limit. On typical local geometries, the conditional probability of four alternating arms with guarded endpoints in those patches is at least $$c\\,A_e(l,L).\n \\label{eq:guarded-arms-lower}$$ The constant depends on the prescribed end layouts and margins, but is independent of the intermediate ratio. In the endpoint overlap regions, requirements use each color coordinate monotonically in its prescribed color order, with no opposite-color requirement on the same coordinate. The entire event is measurable in the annulus with its end margins.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **quad** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *quad* is a bounded Jordan domain $D\subset\mathbb R^2$ with four distinct boundary marks $a,b,c,d$ in counterclockwise order.
- **crossing event C_Q** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a tiling by colored closed cells, let $C_Q$ be the event that the union of the black cells, intersected with $\overline D$, contains a connected subset meeting both closed arcs $ab$ and $cd$.
- **color pivotal site** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A site is *color pivotal* for a crossing if flipping its color, while keeping every other color and the tiling fixed, changes the crossing indicator.
- **black site at density p** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A site, and its cell, is black at density $p$ exactly when $U_v\le p$.
- **four-arm probability A_e** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Write $A_e(r,R)$ for the annealed probability of four alternating arms across a square annulus with inner and outer radii $r,R$.
- **wall** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An interface, or *wall*, crosses each triangle through the edges whose endpoints have different colors.
- **trim collar** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A *trim collar* is a fixed-ratio annulus, or half-annulus in a boundary chart, with a middle circle or semicircle and fixed-factor room on both sides.
- **virtual bank** ([source](#pm-h-16)): We call that changed bank *virtual*, because its original color is restored afterward.
- **guarded endpoint** ([source](#pm-h-30)): A *guarded endpoint* of a path consists of a small box reached by the path, a monochromatic circuit surrounding that box in a larger concentric box and connected to the path, and a reserved further enlargement that every other retained arm is required to avoid, including an arm of the same color.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-poisson-intensity | Poisson intensity | = | $\varepsilon^{-2}$ | Mesh positive |
| q-four-arm-upper | four-arm probability upper bound | ≤ | $C(r/R)^{1+\alpha}$ | Mesh-scale radii |
| q-four-arm-lower | four-arm probability lower bound | ≥ | $c(r/R)^g$ | Mesh-scale radii |
| q-five-arm | polychromatic five-arm probability | ≤ | $C(r/R)^2$ | Mesh-scale radii |
| q-boundary-one-arm | free boundary one-arm probability | ≤ | $C(r/R)^{1/3}$ | Mesh limit first |
| q-boundary-two-arm | free boundary two-arm probability | ≤ | $C(r/R)$ | Mesh limit first |
| q-boundary-three-arm | free boundary three-arm probability | ≤ | $C(r/R)^{4/3}$ | Mesh limit first |
| q-pivotal-scale-rectangle | expected pivotal count of the 2:1 rectangle | ~ | $m_e A_e(b_e,1)$ | Mesh e |
| q-auxiliary-scale | auxiliary scale q_e | ~ | $(m_ea_e)^{-1}$ | Mesh e |
| q-expansion-coefficient | normalized order-k expansion coefficient | ≤ | $C^k k^{-(1-g/2)k}$ | Sufficiently small meshes |
| q-square-transmission | square-insertion kernel | ≥ | $c a_{e,h}$ | h fixed small, e small depending on h |
| q-relative-replacement | relative replacement error | = | $0$ | Ordered limits e then h |
| q-guarded-arms-lower | guarded four-arm conditional probability | ≥ | $c\,A_e(l,L)$ | Fixed ratio before the mesh limit |
| q-square-normalization | limit of q_e times expected unit-square pivotal count | = | $B$ | Mesh to zero |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-cardy-hyp** [claim](#stmt-hyp-cardy) / [proof](#stmt-hyp-cardy): Assumed input -> Annealed Cardy limit
- **s-arm-inputs** [claim](#stmt-lem-arm-inputs) / [proof](#stmt-lem-arm-inputs): Imported critical arm estimates -> Uniform arm bounds
- **s-pivotal-arms** [claim](#stmt-lem-pivotal-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pivotal-arms): Planar duality and trimming -> Necessary arms
- **s-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localization): Occupancy squares and dyadic shells -> Screens
- **s-quenched-cardy** [claim](#stmt-prop-quenched-cardy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quenched-cardy): Efron–Stein with squared arm estimates -> Quenched Cardy
- **s-good-sequences** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-sequences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-sequences): Subsequence principle and Jordan sandwiches -> Deterministic good sequences
- **s-rectangle-colors** [claim](#stmt-lem-rectangle-colors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rectangle-colors): Leftmost path and induction -> Color-order invariance
- **s-color-arms** [claim](#stmt-lem-color-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-color-arms): Strand surgery, BK, BKR and Reimer -> Bulk arm bounds
- **s-chart-cardy** [claim](#stmt-lem-chart-cardy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chart-cardy): Continuum argument and cropping -> Chart Cardy
- **s-trim-arms** [claim](#stmt-lem-trim-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trim-arms): Covering by balls and multi-arm bounds -> Separated trims
- **s-local-guarded-ext** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-guarded-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-guarded-extension): Lasso circuits and Harris inequality -> Extension bound
- **s-boundary-arms** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-arms): Moving-mark Cardy comparison and bootstrap -> Boundary arm exponents
- **s-pivotal-scale** [claim](#stmt-lem-pivotal-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pivotal-scale): Russo formula and arm estimates -> Auxiliary scale
- **s-revealed-faces** [claim](#stmt-lem-revealed-faces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-revealed-faces): Two-connectivity of the band -> Face structure
- **s-exploration-tightness** [claim](#stmt-lem-exploration-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exploration-tightness): Aizenman–Burchard crossing counts -> Tightness
- **s-exploration-contacts** [claim](#stmt-lem-exploration-contacts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exploration-contacts): Arm counts for forbidden contacts -> Contact rules
- **s-face-convergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-face-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-face-convergence): Jordan limits of face boundaries -> Face limits
- **s-stopped-domain** [claim](#stmt-lem-stopped-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stopped-domain): Kernel convergence of side domains -> Stopped target domain
- **s-stopped-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-stopped-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stopped-kernel): Bump tests and Cardy hitting law -> Stopped kernel
- **s-new-tip** [claim](#stmt-lem-new-tip-disconnection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-new-tip-disconnection): Winding-number argument -> No tip disconnection
- **s-no-hidden** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-hidden-excursions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-hidden-excursions): Coupling samples with SLE and excluding excursions -> SLE identification
- **s-chordal** [claim](#stmt-prop-chordal) / [proof](#stmt-prop-chordal): Tightness plus identification -> Chordal convergence
- **s-virtual-restoration** [claim](#stmt-lem-virtual-restoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-virtual-restoration): Incidence identity and limit passage -> Loop completion
- **s-loops** [claim](#stmt-prop-loops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-loops): Finite-step exhaustion of recursion -> Loop convergence
- **s-boundary-pairing** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-pairing): Noncrossing pairing of crosscuts -> Crossing readout
- **s-forced-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-forced-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forced-continuity): Interpolated displacement and arm bounds -> Continuity of tests
- **s-free-segments** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-segments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-segments): Outermost path and six-arm exclusion -> Free segments
- **s-forced-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-forced-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forced-transfer): Polygon witness tubes -> Transfer of prescribed tests
- **s-critical-tests** [claim](#stmt-prop-critical-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-critical-tests): Coupling through ordinary crossings -> Common critical limit
- **s-expansion-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-expansion-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-expansion-bound): Merging forest and leaf integration -> Summable expansion
- **s-location-ui** [claim](#stmt-lem-location-ui) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-location-ui): Higher integrability exponent and Hölder -> Location cutoffs
- **s-guarded-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-guarded-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-guarded-extension): Trim and extend with Harris -> Guarded arm bound
- **s-disk-lower** [claim](#stmt-lem-disk-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-guarded-extension-2): Force square black or white -> Square lower bound
- **s-necklace-sleeve** [claim](#stmt-lem-necklace-sleeve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-necklace-sleeve): Fifth-arm exclusion and hex duality -> Necklace sleeve
- **s-necklace-bit** [claim](#stmt-lem-necklace-bit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-necklace-bit): Hex duality in the necklace disk -> Single transmitted bit
- **s-one-trial** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-trial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-trial): Connector events and Harris -> One-trial comparison
- **s-replacement** [claim](#stmt-prop-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-trial-2): Adaptive exchange involution over many trials -> Signed relative replacement
- **s-common-calibration** [claim](#stmt-lem-common-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-common-calibration): Calibration by first coefficient -> Common constant
- **s-square-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-square-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-square-normalization): First coefficient of unit-square crossing -> Prescribed normalization
- **s-nearcritical-tests** [claim](#stmt-prop-nearcritical-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nearcritical-tests): Expansion termwise limits -> Common finite-time limits
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nearcritical-tests-2): Compactness and second-moment concentration -> Quenched universality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-history-total-mass-and-variation**: For a time vector with T at least the largest absolute time, the signed history measure built from the uniform integral over [-T,T] minus the two constant-history point masses has total mass zero and total variation at most 4T.
- **inv-history-atoms**: In increasing time order the signed history measure puts mass min t_i on the all-one history and -max t_i on the all-zero history; its other nonzero masses are successive differences of the distinct times.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-small-T**: Evaluate the signed history measure with a cutoff T smaller than some absolute time to save work. -> The integral no longer covers every threshold, so the atoms and the zero total mass are wrong.
- **anti-use-geometry-average**: Read the finite history computation as certifying the quenched threshold limit of the Voronoi model on a single mesh. -> The finite measure is only a combinatorial ingredient of the expansion; it says nothing about geometry or about limits.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The main theorem is a weak limit statement and is not finite or constructive, but the paper defines an explicit finite object: the signed history measure on {0,1}^n built from a time vector, with stated atoms, zero total mass and a variation bound. Panel 3 checks only that finite construction.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin, one line of n>=1 space-separated exact rationals t_1..t_n (integers or p/q).
# PRECONDITIONS: every token parses as an exact rational; n>=1.
# OUTPUTS: one line 'bits mass' per nonzero atom of the signed history measure (bits sorted lexicographically, i-th bit is the coordinate of time t_i), then a line 'TV <total variation>'.
# TIME: O(n^2) rational operations after sorting.
# SPACE: O(n^2) characters for the atoms.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Fractions, no floating point.
# FAILURE: empty or unparsable input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    ts = [Fraction(x) for x in sys.stdin.read().split()]
    n = len(ts)
    u = sorted(set(ts))
    mass = {}

    def add(h, m):
        if m != 0:
            mass[h] = mass.get(h, 0) + m

    add('1' * n, u[0])
    add('0' * n, -u[-1])
    for j in range(len(u) - 1):
        h = ''.join('1' if t >= u[j + 1] else '0' for t in ts)
        add(h, u[j + 1] - u[j])
    for h in sorted(mass):
        print(h, mass[h])
    print('TV', sum(abs(v) for v in mass.values()))


main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef parse(txt):\n    d = {}\n    tv = None\n    for line in txt.splitlines():\n        p = line.split()\n        if not p:\n            continue\n        if p[0] == 'TV':\n            tv = Fraction(p[1])\n        else:\n            d[p[0]] = Fraction(p[1])\n    return d, tv\n\n\ndef truth(ts):\n    n = len(ts)\n    T = max(abs(t) for t in ts)\n    pts = sorted(set(ts) | {-T, T})\n    acc = {}\n    for a, b in zip(pts, pts[1:]):\n        h = ''.join('1' if b <= t else '0' for t in ts)\n        acc[h] = acc.get(h, 0) + (b - a)\n    for h in ('1' * n, '0' * n):\n        acc[h] = acc.get(h, 0) - T\n    return {h: m for h, m in acc.items() if m != 0}\n\n\ndef main():\n    ts = [Fraction(x) for x in open(sys.argv[1]).read().split()]\n    cand, ctv = parse(open(sys.argv[2]).read())\n    exp, etv = parse(sys.argv[3])\n    ref = truth(ts)\n    T = max(abs(t) for t in ts)\n    tv = sum(abs(m) for m in ref.values())\n    ok = (cand == ref and exp == ref and ctv == tv and etv == tv\n          and sum(ref.values()) == 0 and tv <= 4 * T)\n    sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)\n\n\nmain()\n",
    "output_contract": "Lines 'bits mass' for every nonzero atom of the signed history measure in lexicographic order of bits, then 'TV value' with the total variation; masses are exact rationals.",
    "reason": "The main theorem is a weak limit statement and is not finite or constructive, but the paper defines an explicit finite object: the signed history measure on {0,1}^n built from a time vector, with stated atoms, zero total mass and a variation bound. Panel 3 checks only that finite construction.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: stdin, one line of n>=1 space-separated exact rationals t_1..t_n (integers or p/q).\n# PRECONDITIONS: every token parses as an exact rational; n>=1.\n# OUTPUTS: one line 'bits mass' per nonzero atom of the signed history measure (bits sorted lexicographically, i-th bit is the coordinate of time t_i), then a line 'TV <total variation>'.\n# TIME: O(n^2) rational operations after sorting.\n# SPACE: O(n^2) characters for the atoms.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact Fractions, no floating point.\n# FAILURE: empty or unparsable input raises an exception and exits nonzero.\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef main():\n    ts = [Fraction(x) for x in sys.stdin.read().split()]\n    n = len(ts)\n    u = sorted(set(ts))\n    mass = {}\n\n    def add(h, m):\n        if m != 0:\n            mass[h] = mass.get(h, 0) + m\n\n    add('1' * n, u[0])\n    add('0' * n, -u[-1])\n    for j in range(len(u) - 1):\n        h = ''.join('1' if t >= u[j + 1] else '0' for t in ts)\n        add(h, u[j + 1] - u[j])\n    for h in sorted(mass):\n        print(h, mass[h])\n    print('TV', sum(abs(v) for v in mass.values()))\n\n\nmain()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "TV 0",
        "input": "0"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0 -1\n1 1\nTV 2",
        "input": "1"
      },
      {
        "expected": "00 -2\n01 1\n11 1\nTV 4",
        "input": "1 2"
      },
      {

        "input": "-1 1 0"
      },

        "input": "1/2 1/2"

        "input": "3 -3"
      },
      {

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves weak-convergence statements for crossing thresholds and supplies no procedure, interface or finite computation that a program could execute. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a limit law as the mesh tends to zero, with no rate and no explicit mesh at which a simulated Voronoi map matches the triangular reference law. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No measurement, checking or conversion procedure is stated; the pivotal-count normalization is defined through an expectation, not computed by a stated method. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper is an algorithm with steps, inputs and outputs; the exploration procedure is a proof device whose limit is identified, not a computational method with a cost analysis. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no representation or index; the triangulation and revealed graph are analysis objects, not structures with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no kernel, dataflow or numerical routine that could be mapped to hardware; the signed site expansion is a convergence argument, not a computation to parallelize. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no hardness assumption, adversary model or security property; percolation thresholds of a random tessellation do not give a cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or generalization claim appears; the concentration of the conditional threshold law concerns tessellation geometry, not a trained model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective, constraint set or optimality statement; thresholds are random variables, not decision variables, and no rate is given for choosing a mesh. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model is a static colored planar tessellation with no messages, failures or consensus; connectivity of colored cells is not a protocol guarantee. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only transferable content is theoretical: a hypothesis-conditional universality statement for planar percolation, with no executable or engineering construction attached. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-cardy-input-assumed** (hypothesis): Every conclusion for the Voronoi model is conditional on the scalar annealed Cardy formula, which the paper takes as its sole supplied scaling-limit input and does not prove. Blocked use: ['Citing the quenched near-critical universality as unconditional without importing a proof of the Cardy input', 'Applying the result to a tessellation model for which the scalar Cardy limit is unavailable']
- **limit-planar-conformal-scope** (formal_scope): The input is formulated for bounded Jordan quads through a conformal map to the upper half-plane, so the whole framework is planar. Blocked use: ['Transferring the limit law or the normalization to three-dimensional or non-planar tessellations', 'Using the Cardy hypothesis for unbounded or non-Jordan domains']
- **limit-quenched-in-probability** (formal_scope): The Voronoi statement is convergence in probability over the tessellation, with color marks averaged and geometry kept fixed; it is not almost sure convergence for each realization. Blocked use: ['Claiming that every individual sampled tessellation has a threshold law close to the triangular reference', 'Reading the conditional law as an average over geometry']
- **limit-ineffective-constants** (unknown): The arm estimates hold with constants and exponents that are asserted to exist but are not given explicitly, and no rate of convergence is stated for the main limit. Blocked use: ['Deriving explicit error bounds or a mesh size at which the Voronoi threshold law is within a stated distance of the reference', 'Using the arm exponents as numerical values beyond the stated ranges']
- **limit-subsequence-extraction** (nonconstructive): Deterministic good sequences are obtained by extracting subsequences and coupling geometries on a common probability space, so the good geometries are not exhibited by any explicit rule. Blocked use: ['Presenting the extraction as a procedure that produces a good tessellation for a given mesh', 'Using the deterministic-sequence results without passing to a subsequence and back via the convergence-in-probability criterion']
- **limit-mesh-first-order** (approximation): Fixed-scale arm and crossing estimates are limits superior in the mesh with the scales fixed first, so they carry no uniformity once scales are allowed to shrink with the mesh. Blocked use: ['Applying fixed-scale arm bounds at radii that decrease together with the mesh', 'Interchanging the order of the mesh limit and the radius limit']
- **limit-two-replica-expansion** (hypothesis): The uniform bound on the site expansion is proved for at most two color replicas, finitely many polygonal tests and a fixed box. Blocked use: ['Extending the absolute summability claim to three or more replicas sharing one geometry', 'Using the bound for an unbounded family of tests or an unbounded box']
- **limit-finite-tests-rational-quads** (edge_case): The common finite-time limits concern finitely many rational polygonal quads and finitely many real parameters; the full law is obtained only through projections on a countable index set. Blocked use: ['Reading the finite-time limit as a uniform statement over all quads or all parameters at once', 'Applying it to quads with non-polygonal or irrational boundary without an approximation argument']
- **limit-normalization-averages-geometry** (formal_scope): The normalizing scale for the Voronoi model is an expectation that averages geometry as well as colors, so it is a deterministic quantity and not a per-tessellation pivotal count. Blocked use: ['Normalizing a single sampled tessellation by its own pivotal count and expecting the same limit', 'Estimating the scale from one realization without arguing concentration']
- **limit-closed-cell-convention** (edge_case): Crossings are defined by closed black cells inside the closed domain with no boundary wiring, so results depend on this convention and on the stated general-position treatment. Blocked use: ['Comparing the thresholds with a crossing convention that uses boundary wiring or open cells without a separate argument']
- **limit-local-change-arms** (hypothesis): The necessary-arm statement compares two tilings that agree outside a ball and throughout an intervening annulus, and all pivotal arm costs are derived under that agreement. Blocked use: ['Applying the arm necessity to color changes that are not confined to a ball with an agreement annulus', 'Ignoring other prescribed local changes that are not held fixed']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Taking Cardy's crossing formula for critical Poisson–Voronoi percolation as an input, we prove a universal joint limit for the near-critical crossing thresholds of rational polygonal quadrilaterals under the monotone coupling. Conditional on the Poisson tessellation, the threshold law converges in probability over tessellations to the same law as on the triangular lattice. Each model is normalized by its own expected number of color-pivotal sites for a unit-square crossing.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Critical crossing probabilities describe the large-scale geometry of planar percolation at one density.
